- **wiresmith-migrator**: Migrates a Go repository from gogoproto-generated protobuf code to wiresmith. Use when starting, continuing, or resuming a gogo→wiresmith migration in a consumer repo (e.g. mimir/tempo/loki): annotating .proto files, wiring the wiresmith generator into the build, bridging mixed gogo/wiresmith state with shims, and verifying each step. Give it a scoped target — a proto cluster, or "next unit of the migration". It works leaf-first in atomic verified commits, maintains WIRESMITH_MIGRATION.md, and files wiresmith beads for tool gaps instead of silently working around them. ([grafana/wiresmith](https://github.com/grafana/wiresmith/blob/HEAD/agents/wiresmith-migrator.md) · ★27)

It never writes to Workato: edits, starts, stops, tags and deployments are made in the main conversation. Give it one recipe or one operation per call. Not for design decisions or open-ended investigation. ([Phalconline/workato-kit](https://github.com/Phalconline/workato-kit/blob/HEAD/.claude/agents/workato-operator.md))
- **workato-qa**: Independent QA Engineer of the virtual Workato team. Use to validate automation specs and recipe JSON (local checker + Workato server validators) and to run and judge acceptance tests. Writes verdicts and reports; never modifies recipes or starts/stops anything. ([aniketpitre/Workato-Claude-MCP](https://github.com/aniketpitre/Workato-Claude-MCP/blob/HEAD/.claude/agents/workato-qa.md))
- **workato-recipe-dev**: Integration Developer of the virtual Workato team. Use at the Build stage to write recipe JSON from an approved design (adapting the cited proven examples), self-check it with tools/recipe_check.py, and at the Push stage to create or update recipes STOPPED in the dev project and verify them by read-back diff. Never starts recipes. ([aniketpitre/Workato-Claude-MCP](https://github.com/aniketpitre/Workato-Claude-MCP/blob/HEAD/.claude/agents/workato-recipe-dev.md))

- **workbench-validator**: Validates super-review workbench changes with check, lint, and optional cli/coverage/spec. Use after editing repository tooling, docs, CI, the CLI package, or tests. ([martinthommesen/super-review](https://github.com/martinthommesen/super-review/blob/HEAD/.claude/agents/workbench-validator.md))

- **worker-deep**: Hard implementation in a fresh Fable context: tasks that need investigation, design decisions, or cross-module changes — root-causing a failure and fixing it, building something where the approach isn't decided yet, changes whose blast radius must be understood first. Give it the goal, constraints, and what done looks like; it owns the how. For bounded changes where the approach is already decided, use worker-light. - **worker-dev**: Code-writing sub-agent dispatched by loop-self-healing (or loop-orchestrator for fan-out scenarios). Reads the spec, reads the example file from §6, writes ONE assigned file in its own git worktree (NUEVO or MODIFICAR), and returns. Never runs the project's verification suite — that's the runtime's job. Receives explicit failure context on retry rounds and is instructed to fix only what the error names. Language-agnostic — supports any stack the spec was written against; consults references/universal-commands.md to discover the project's own commands rather than assuming Node/Jest.
